# Outlex data model (Phase 0–1 web MVP)

Status: **Draft for owner review, 2026-11-07.** Code name Outlet NK. Not legal advice. Items marked ⚠ are VERIFY BEFORE LAUNCH and map to rows in `docs/compliance/verify-register.md` (V-numbers).

Sources, highest precedence first: `docs/BUILD-PROMPT.md` → `docs/outlet-nk-vision-addendum.md` → `docs/haul-master-prompt-v3.md` (HAUL = Outlex). Also applied: ADR-0006 (global-ready), `docs/specs/price-watch.md`, `docs/specs/shipping-domestic-international.md`, `docs/design/ui-reference-spec.md`, `docs/open-questions.md`, and the scale and security research notes. Reference only: the older iOS-first model in the `OUTLET` repo (good parts reused and adapted).

This document is a **design, not a deployed schema**. No migrations exist yet. Companion files: `data_inventory.yaml` (repo root, every column tagged) and `docs/privacy/ropa.md` (records of processing). The column tables in §7, the inventory and the RoPA are produced from **one schema source**, so the three never disagree on table or column names, PII class or residency. That source also runs these checks: no personal data in a global table, every P3 column encrypted, every money column is `bigint` with a currency, every store-tenant table has `store_id`, every cell table has `market_id` (or a written reason), and every foreign key points at a known table.

**Size:** 209 tables, 2142 columns (271 P0, 1643 P1, 207 P2, 21 P3). 99 tables hold personal data. Residency: 150 `cell`, 26 `cell_publishable`, 33 `global`. 57 tables are store-tenant (RLS), 49 are user-owned (RLS). 29 tables are partitioned. By phase: 45 Phase 0, 163 Phase 1, 1 Phase 2 (designed now because a spec names it). Other Phase 2/3 entities are sketched in §8.

## 1. How to read this

- §2 conventions apply to every table. Module sections repeat only what differs.
- §3 lists the modelling decisions I defaulted, each with the options compared. Each should become an ADR line or be folded into ADR-0001/0005/0006.
- §4 is the cross-module spine. §5 is capacity and partitioning. §7 has one section per module: intro, state machines, a mermaid ER diagram (keys, tenant and status columns only), then every table.
- In the table header line: *residency* (§2.4), *tenant* (§2.5), *basis* (lawful basis, proposed), *retention* (rule key in `data_inventory.yaml`).
- In index lines, `-> text` names the query the index supports.

## 2. Conventions

### 2.1 Identifiers, types, time
| Topic | Rule |
|---|---|
| Primary keys | `uuid`, **UUIDv7** generated by the server (time-ordered, good B-tree locality). Not a security control: authorization is policy + RLS (BUILD-PROMPT §4). Natural keys only for reference data (`markets.id = 'EG'`, `currencies.code`, `roles.code`). |
| Share tokens, passport codes, voucher codes | 128-bit CSPRNG, never UUIDv7 (it leaks time). Secret tokens are stored only as a hash; public passport codes are stored plain because the page is public. |
| Money | `*_minor bigint` in the currency's minor unit plus `currency char(3)` on the row (ADR-0006 #2). No floats, no `numeric` for money. No mixed-currency sums. One documented banker's-rounding point. Rates and percentages are basis points (`*_bp int`, 1400 = 14%). |
| Weights and sizes | Integer grams (`*_g`) and millimetres (`*_mm`), so chargeable-weight maths stays integer. |
| Time | `timestamptz` in UTC. Business windows (Drop Hour, quiet hours, return windows) are computed in the market time zone from `markets.timezone` with an injected clock. Calendar-only facts use `date`. |
| Text | Arabic columns (`*_ar`) are mandatory for customer-facing content; `*_en` is optional (CPL 181/2018, master prompt A2.15). |
| Enumerations | `text` + CHECK generated from the Zod enum in `packages/domain`. |
| JSONB | Only for documents validated by a named Zod schema (attributes, rules, quotes, config values). Never for references. Bounded arrays (`text[]`, `uuid[]` up to 20) only for immutable lists. |
| Soft delete | `deleted_at` only where stated. Financial and tax rows are never deleted; they are pseudonymised (V77). |
| Versions | Config, plans, rate cards, agreements, defect cards and templates are immutable per version. |

### 2.2 `market_id` everywhere (ADR-0006)
Every market-scoped row carries `market_id text` referencing `markets(id)` (ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, `EG` at launch). Child rows that are always read through their parent (for example `postings`, `order_lines`) inherit the market and are marked *nomarket* with the reason in the inventory. Country rules are `config_entries` rows, never code branches. Money accounts are per market and currency.

### 2.3 PII classes
| Class | Meaning | Storage |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | Public | Clear. May be cached on the CDN and projected to the global plane. |
| P1 | Internal, not personal on its own | Clear. |
| P2 | Personal | Clear or encrypted as tagged. Access logged; in DSAR export and erasure scope. A column holding a user, device or staff id is P2. |
| P3 | Sensitive | KMS envelope encryption per field (`*_ct bytea`, key id in the header), HMAC blind index (`*_bidx`) for equality lookup, cell only, never sent to an external LLM, never shown to sellers unless a rule says so. P3 here is an engineering class; which fields are "sensitive data" under PDPL is ⚠ V73. |

A table is personal data as a whole when it has any P2/P3 column or a data subject. The class of a column describes its own content; the table's lawful basis and retention apply to all of its columns.

### 2.4 Residency classes (hybrid residency, ADR-0002 draft, ADR-0006 #6)
| Class | Where it lives | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `cell` | Only in the market's cell (EG cell in Egypt) | All personal, money and stock data. Leaves only through a licensed transfer (V2, V30, V39). |
| `cell_publishable` | Source of truth in the cell | Only **P0 columns** may be projected to the global public plane, and only through `outbox_events` with `visibility = 'public'`. A CI contract test checks public event schemas against this inventory. |
| `global` | Global control plane | Config, taxonomies, public reference data. **Must hold no personal data** (generator check). Actor trails for global config go to the cell `audit_log`. |

The global plane (Next.js rendering, search engine, CDN images, sitemaps) is fed only by public events. Price and stock on public pages are dynamic holes read from the cell (TTL ≤ 5 s), never from the global plane.

### 2.5 Tenancy and row-level security
| Tenant column | Applies to | Mechanism |
|---|---|---|
| `store_id` | Every seller-owned row | `ALTER TABLE … FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY`; policy `store_id = current_setting('app.current_store_id')::uuid`. The API connects as a **non-owner role without BYPASSRLS** and sets the store with `SET LOCAL` inside each transaction (PgBouncer transaction mode leaks session settings). One active store per request; the Seller Center switcher changes it. |
| `user_id` | Shopper-owned rows | Policy `user_id = current_setting('app.current_user_id')::uuid`. |
| both | Rows a shopper and a seller both see (`seller_orders`, `order_lines`, `return_requests`, `shipments`, `disputes`, `see_it_first_requests`) | Two permissive policies. Shopper delivery PII reaches sellers only through a view gated by `delivery_snapshots.seller_access_until`. |
| none | Config, ledger, kernel | No tenant policy; access by database role and module port. Seller finance views come through payouts and orders, never direct ledger reads. |

Staff use a separate `app_admin` role whose policies require a reason code; every P2/P3 reveal writes `pii_access_log`. Background workers use `app_worker` with narrow policies per job, never BYPASSRLS. **CI:** an automatic RLS test for every tenant table and a negative authorization test for every endpoint (other tenant → 404, wrong role → 403). A missing object and a foreign object both return 404.

### 2.6 Indexes, pagination, query rules
- Every WHERE, JOIN and ORDER BY in application code has a supporting index, proven with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` in the PR; indexes are created `CONCURRENTLY` in expand/contract migrations.
- **Keyset pagination only**, max page size 50, deterministic tie-breaker: `ORDER BY created_at DESC, id DESC` with cursor `(created_at, id)`. Because ids are UUIDv7, lists ordered purely by time may use `id DESC` alone.
- Partial indexes for queues and sweepers (`WHERE status = …`), so the hot part stays small. Their write cost is noted where a table is hot (`inventory_units` uses fillfactor 70 for HOT updates).
- No `SELECT *`. Money and stock reads go to the primary; catalog reads may use replicas (lag alert at 1 s).

### 2.7 Partitioning
- Spec-mandated monthly partitions: events (outbox, inbox, notifications, analytics), snapshots, webhooks and audit. `channel_snapshots` is daily (spec).
- **UUIDv7 range partitioning on the key** (decision DM-05) where a key must be globally unique: the same key always maps to the same partition, so a plain primary key still guarantees uniqueness and old partitions are dropped for retention.
- Provider-supplied ids (not UUIDv7) are deduplicated in the small unpartitioned `dedup_keys`.
- Order and money aggregates are not partitioned in Phase 1 (DM-12); `journal_entries` and `postings` are (aligned bounds).

### 2.8 Idempotency, events, state machines
- Every mutating route takes an `Idempotency-Key` (UUIDv7) stored for 24 h in `idempotency_keys` (same key + different body hash → 422). **Permanent** uniqueness for money and orders lives in business keys: `journal_entry_keys`, `order_groups.checkout_session_id`, `payment_intents.order_group_id`, `refunds.idempotency_key`, `payouts.idempotency_key`, `reservations.idempotency_key`, `shipments.idempotency_key`.
- Domain events are `outbox_events` rows in the same transaction as the state change; consumers record `inbox_processed` in the same transaction as their effect (at-least-once, never "exactly once").
- Every state machine has one owning table and column, a table-driven definition in `packages/domain`, and a typed error on an invalid transition. Machines are listed in each module intro.

## 3. Modelling decisions I defaulted

Each compares at least two options. None is irreversible in the A4 sense (money, legal, hosting and provider choices stay open owner decisions); each is reversible by an expand/contract migration.

| ID | Decision (default) | Options compared | Why (2–3 lines) |
|---|---|---|---|
| DM-01 | `market_id` is `text` ISO code (`'EG'`) referencing `markets` | (a) UUID surrogate (b) ISO code | ISO codes are stable, readable in logs and RLS, and two bytes smaller in every composite index. A future sub-national market can use a suffix (`AE-DXB`) without a type change. |
| DM-02 | Shopper accounts live **per market cell**; no global user directory | (a) global identity table (b) per-cell accounts | A global directory, even of blind indexes, puts personal data outside Egypt and needs a transfer licence (V2, V39). Per-cell accounts keep the hybrid boundary clean; cross-market login can be a later federated feature (V30). |
| DM-03 | Physical, serialised units for defect passports (`item_units`) are **separate** from the reservation pool (`inventory_units`) | (a) one table (b) two tables | The reservation pool is a capped, replenished abstraction (Shopify pattern) whose claimed rows are archived or deleted (ADR-0005). Passports need a stable physical identity and a public QR code. Graded units link pool rows through `item_unit_id`. |
| DM-04 | Product attributes = **JSONB validated by the category attribute set** + GIN index + generated columns for hot facets | (a) EAV rows (b) typed column per attribute (c) JSONB | Multi-category catalogs change attribute sets often. EAV makes every listing read an N-row join; typed columns need migrations per category. JSONB plus a Zod set keeps reads to one row; search does faceting, Postgres is the fallback. |
| DM-05 | Partition by **UUIDv7 range on the key** where uniqueness must hold; ledger entries and postings share bounds | (a) partition by `created_at` + separate unique tables (b) UUIDv7 range on id | (a) breaks global uniqueness and forces lookups across all partitions. (b) keeps PK uniqueness, prunes `WHERE id = $1` to one partition, and lets entries and postings co-locate. Caveat: ids must be server-generated at insert; client keys outside ±24 h are rejected. |
| DM-06 | **Both funds-flow models** are modelled; each order group snapshots `funds_flow` | (a) pick PSP split now (b) pick merchant-collect now (c) support both | Q2 is open and depends on Paymob/Kashier answers and counsel (V3, V4). The ledger chart is identical; only settlement differs (`payment_split_instructions` vs `payouts.mode = transfer`). Cost: two small tables and one column. |
| DM-07 | **Seller** = KYC'd legal entity; **store** = storefront per market and the tenant key | (a) tenant = seller (b) tenant = store | Stores carry market, slug, display mode and plan; a seller may run several markets later (ADR-0006 #11). Staff permissions and RLS are per store, which matches the Seller Center store switcher. |
| DM-08 | Condition grade and **defect card on the listing**, immutable versions, acknowledgement stored with the version on cart and order lines | (a) per SKU (b) per listing (c) per unit only | The UI spec says a different condition is a separate listing. Versioning makes "I have seen the N defects" provable in a dispute (V19). Per-unit passports point at the version the unit was graded with. |
| DM-09 | Shoppers and seller staff share `users`; **admins are a separate `staff_accounts` principal** | (a) one table for all (b) three tables (c) users + staff | Brand owners also shop, so one identity avoids duplicate phones. Admin access has different auth (SSO + passkey, IP allow-list) and different retention, and must never inherit shopper sessions. |
| DM-10 | Discount display mode is a **store setting** (`public` default, `member_only`, `hidden`); the effective result is precomputed in `sku_price_state` | (a) per listing (b) per store (c) per store with listing override | Addendum C4 and D3 describe a brand option. Per store is simple to explain and audit; a listing override can be added later. Precomputing keeps PDP and shelf reads to one row, and an unverified claim never shows in any mode. |
| DM-11 | Commission resolved by rule priority and **snapshotted per seller order**; plan fees **netted from payouts** by default | (a) live lookup (b) snapshot; (c) card on file (d) netting | Snapshots keep past orders stable when D1 prices change. Netting needs no stored card for sellers and fits both funds-flow models; a bank-transfer invoice is the fallback (ADR-0008). |
| DM-12 | Order tables **not partitioned** in Phase 1; switch at about 100M rows | (a) partition now (b) later by expand/contract | About 12–30M seller orders per year at 4M users (estimate). Partitioning now complicates global uniques and FKs for little gain; id-range partitioning can be introduced with dual writes when measurements require it. |
| DM-13 | `channel_snapshots` partitioned **daily** | (a) monthly default (b) daily (spec) | Watched SKUs re-check hourly; daily partitions keep each partition small and make the 13-month drop cheap. Evidence used by a claim is copied into `price_evidence`, so snapshots can expire. |
| DM-14 | Promotion budgets in **16 shards** | (a) one counter row (b) sharded counters | A single counter row becomes a lock hotspot at Drop Hour. Shards spread writes; the cap is checked per shard with borrowing under an advisory lock. |
| DM-15 | Audit and ledger hash chains are **sealed asynchronously** (Merkle seals every minute or batch) | (a) prev-hash on every row in the write path (b) async seals | (a) serialises every writer on one chain head. (b) keeps rows append-only and writers parallel, and still detects any deleted or changed row on verification. |
| DM-16 | Public reviews show "Verified buyer", no name by default | (a) show nickname (b) anonymous by default with opt-in | Showing names would send personal data to the global plane and needs a transfer basis (V2). Rating, fit and size bought carry the value. |
| DM-18 | `ledger_accounts.id` is a UUID; the chart's text id is a UNIQUE `code` | (a) text id as PK (chart doc) (b) UUID PK + unique code | `postings` is the biggest money table; a 16-byte key instead of a ~60-byte string keeps its indexes small. The chart's readable id is still enforced and used in logs and reports. The chart doc needs a one-line note to match. |
| DM-17 | One schema source generates the ERD tables, the inventory and the RoPA | (a) hand-written files (b) generated from one source | The older repo needed an inconsistency register for model–inventory drift. Generation removes that class of error; Phase 0b turns the source into the migration-side CI check. |

## 4. Cross-module spine

The diagram shows the main relationships only. Module diagrams in §7 show each area.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    markets ||--o{ stores : hosts
    markets ||--o{ users : hosts
    sellers ||--o{ stores : operates
    stores ||--o{ store_members : staffed_by
    users ||--o{ store_members : works_as
    stores ||--o{ products : lists
    products ||--o{ skus : variants
    products ||--o| defect_cards : current_card
    defect_cards ||--o{ defect_items : lists
    defect_cards ||--o{ item_units : graded_with
    skus ||--o{ inventory_levels : stocked_at
    inventory_levels ||--o{ inventory_units : pool
    item_units ||--o| inventory_units : backs
    skus ||--o{ price_events : priced_by
    skus ||--|| sku_price_state : displays
    channel_connections ||--o{ external_products : imports
    external_products ||--o{ product_matches : matched_to
    skus ||--o{ product_matches : matched_by
    users ||--o{ carts : has
    carts ||--o{ cart_lines : contains
    users ||--o{ order_groups : places
    order_groups ||--|{ seller_orders : splits
    seller_orders ||--|{ order_lines : contains
    order_lines ||--o{ order_line_units : sold_unit
    order_groups ||--|| payment_intents : paid_by
    order_groups ||--o{ payment_tenders : funded_by
    seller_orders ||--o{ shipment_seller_orders : shipped_in
    shipments ||--o{ shipment_seller_orders : carries
    seller_orders ||--o{ return_requests : returned_as
    order_groups ||--o{ refunds : refunded_by
    journal_entries ||--|{ postings : balances
    ledger_accounts ||--o{ postings : receives
    ledger_accounts ||--o{ ledger_holds : reserves
    seller_orders ||--o| payout_accruals : accrues
    payouts ||--o{ payout_items : composed_of
    users ||--o{ credit_lots : owns
    seller_orders ||--o{ tax_documents : evidenced_by
    stores ||--o{ seller_plan_subscriptions : subscribes
```

**Module dependency direction** (acyclic, enforced by dependency-cruiser): kernel/markets/flags/admin/auth/privacy/ledger → sellers, catalog, price_watch, search, seo, tax rates → inventory, cart, promotions, risk, orders → payments, store_credit, payouts → shipping, returns → notifications, analytics, engagement. `promotions`, `risk` ranking and seller modules may not read budget or measurement data. `ledger` is written only through `LedgerPort`.

## 5. Capacity and partition plan (estimates)

Assumptions (BUILD-PROMPT §3, unverified, replace with k6 and production numbers): 4M registered users, about 400k daily users, about 10% ordering per month, 1.4 stores per order, 2.5 lines per seller order, about 2M live SKUs at maturity.

| Table | Rows per month (estimate) | Plan |
|---|---|---|
| `order_groups` / `seller_orders` / `order_lines` | 0.4M / 0.56M / 1.4M | Unpartitioned in Phase 1 (DM-12) |
| `journal_entries` / `postings` | about 1.5M / 6M | Monthly by UUIDv7 range, aligned |
| `outbox_events` / `inbox_processed` | about 30M / 60–90M | Monthly by UUIDv7 range; drop after 60 days |
| `audit_log` | about 10M | Monthly; 7-year retention, sealed |
| `price_events` | about 5M | Monthly by `valid_from` |
| `channel_snapshots` | 5–20M per **day** at maturity | Daily; drop after 13 months |
| `search_queries` | about 60M | Monthly; 25 months |
| `analytics_events` | 300M+ | ClickHouse monthly partitions, TTL 25 months |
| `inventory_units` | bounded: ≤ 1,000 per SKU-location pool | Not partitioned; kept small by compaction |
| `notification_requests` / `deliveries` | 20M / 30M | Monthly; 12 months |

Connection pools (BUILD-PROMPT §3): separate PgBouncer pools for `checkout.reserve`, `catalog.read` and `analytics`; pools × size × instances < 0.8 × `max_connections`.

## 7. Modules

<a id="m-markets"></a>
### 7.1 Markets, cells and configuration

Every market-scoped row carries `market_id` (ADR-0006). A **market** is a country storefront with its own currency, languages, time zone, tax rules and legal windows. A **cell** is the physical home of a market's personal, money and stock data (EG cell in Egypt, hybrid residency per the ADR-0002 draft). Country rules are configuration rows, never `if (EG)` code. Config is versioned and immutable per version; the actor trail of every config change goes to the cell `audit_log`, so global tables hold no personal data.

**Config keys used by Phase 1** (namespace.key, Zod-validated per key in `packages/config`): `returns.no_reason_days` (14), `returns.defect_days` (30, V19), `cancel.distance_selling_days` (7, V7), `age.shop_min` (18), `age.contract_min` (21, V8), `quiet_hours.start/end` (23:00-09:00 local), `cod.first_order_cap_minor`, `cod.confirm_within_minutes` (120), `delivery.free_threshold_minor` (200000 = 2,000 EGP), `price.reference_window_days` (30), `price.min_claim_bp` (500), `price.match_tau_bp` (9200, V13), `price.evidence_stale_hours` (72), `drop.hour_local` ("21:00", after iftar in Ramadan from the calendar), `payments.funds_flow` (`psp_split` or `collect_payout`, Q2), `reservation.ttl.card_s` (600), `reservation.ttl.wallet_s` (1800), `reservation.ttl.fawry_s` (86400), `discount.display_default` (`public`, D3).

```mermaid
erDiagram

        text id PK

    }
    markets {
        text id PK
        text cell_id FK
        text status
    }
    currencies {
        char(3) code PK
    }

    }

    }

        text market_id FK
        uuid parent_id FK
    }
    cells ||--o{ markets : "cell_id"
```

#### `cells`
Phase 0 · residency `global` · tenant none · no personal data · basis `not_personal_data` · retention `config_versions` · not partitioned

Physical data homes. Routing layer maps market -> cell.

| Column | Type | Null | PII | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | text PK | no | P0 | e.g. 'eg-1' |
| `provider` | text | no | P1 | e.g. huawei_cairo (V37) |
| `region_code` | text | no | P1 | provider region id |
| `country_code` | char(2) | no | P0 | ISO 3166-1 where the data physically lives |
| `status` | text | no | P1 | planned, live, draining |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | P1 | UTC |
- ⚠ VERIFY: V37

Phase 0 · residency `global` · tenant none · no personal data · basis `not_personal_data` · retention `config_versions` · not partitioned

| `id` | text PK | no | P0 | ISO 3166-1 alpha-2, 'EG' at launch; natural key (decision DM-01) |
| `cell_id` | text | no | P1 | -> `cells`. where this market's personal and money data lives |

<a id="m-kernel"></a>
### 7.2 Platform kernel: outbox, inbox, idempotency, dedup, sagas, audit, media, keys

Shared plumbing every module uses. Delivery is **at-least-once**; every consumer is idempotent through `inbox_processed` (master prompt A2.7). Uniqueness that must hold across partitions uses **UUIDv7 range partitioning on the key itself** (decision DM-05): the same key always lands in the same partition, so a plain primary key stays globally unique and old partitions can be dropped.

**Outbox visibility rule (residency boundary).** `outbox_events.visibility = 'public'` events carry only P0 columns and are the *only* thing the relay may send to the global plane (catalog read models, search, CDN revalidation, sitemaps). A CI contract test asserts that public event schemas contain only P0 fields from `data_inventory.yaml`.

**Audit hash chain at scale.** Writers append to `audit_log` with a `row_hash` and never update. A single sealer job (not the write path, so writers are never serialised on one hash head) writes `audit_seals` every minute: `seal_hash = H(prev_seal_hash || merkle_root(row_hashes in id order))`, signs it with the audit key, and ships it to WORM storage. Verification recomputes row hashes and seals.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    outbox_events {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    inbox_processed {
        text consumer PK
        uuid message_id PK
    }
    idempotency_keys {
        uuid key PK
        uuid actor_id PK
        text route PK
        text status
    }
    dedup_keys {
        text namespace PK
        text key PK
    }
    saga_instances {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text state
    }
    dlq_messages {
        uuid id PK
        text status
    }
    audit_log {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    audit_seals {
        uuid id PK
    }
    media_assets {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
    }
    crypto_keys {
        text id PK
        text status
    }
    stores ||--o{ media_assets : "store_id"
```

<a id="m-flags"></a>
### 7.3 Feature flags and kill switches

OpenFeature with a self-hosted provider. Flags and rules are global configuration. **Targeting never stores user ids** in global tables: rules target markets, stores (business ids), segments or a percentage hash. Every toggle is written to the cell `audit_log`. Kill switches exist for each payment method, COD, BNPL, drops, crawler, LLM features and international shipping (BUILD-PROMPT §8).

```mermaid
erDiagram
    feature_flags {
        text key PK
    }
    flag_rules {
        uuid id PK
        text flag_key FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    feature_flags ||--o{ flag_rules : "flag_key"
```

<a id="m-admin"></a>
### 7.4 Admin staff, RBAC and dual approval

Admin staff are a **separate principal** from shoppers and seller staff (decision DM-09): own accounts, SSO plus mandatory passkey, IP allow-list for Finance and Super-admin. Roles and permissions are global definitions; assignments, approvals and sessions are personal data and live in the cell. Dual approval covers payouts and refunds above a threshold, plan price changes, role grants, data exports, money/legal config keys and ledger adjustments. `requested_by <> decided_by` is a CHECK constraint.

**State machines.** **ApprovalRequest**: `PENDING -> APPROVED -> EXECUTED`, `PENDING -> REJECTED | EXPIRED`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    staff_accounts {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    staff_passkeys {
        uuid id PK
        uuid staff_id FK
    }
    roles {
        text code PK
    }
    permissions {
        text code PK
    }
    role_permissions {
        text role_code PK,FK
        text permission_code PK,FK
    }
    staff_role_assignments {
        uuid id PK
        uuid staff_id FK
        text role_code FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid granted_by FK
        uuid approval_request_id FK
    }
    staff_sessions {
        uuid id PK
        uuid staff_id FK
    }
    approval_requests {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid requested_by FK
        text status
        uuid decided_by FK
    }
    approval_requests ||--o{ staff_role_assignments : "approval_request_id"
    permissions ||--o{ role_permissions : "permission_code"
    roles ||--o{ role_permissions : "role_code"
    roles ||--o{ staff_role_assignments : "role_code"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ approval_requests : "decided_by"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ approval_requests : "requested_by"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ staff_passkeys : "staff_id"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ staff_role_assignments : "granted_by"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ staff_role_assignments : "staff_id"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ staff_sessions : "staff_id"
```

<a id="m-auth"></a>
### 7.5 Identity, sessions and devices

One identity table for **shoppers and seller staff** (a brand owner can also shop); seller capabilities need an active `store_members` row and a passkey in the session `amr` (decision DM-09). Accounts are **per market cell**: there is no global user directory, so no personal data sits in the global plane (decision DM-02). Phone is the account key: E.164, envelope-encrypted, with an HMAC blind index for the unique lookup. Access JWT 10 min; refresh tokens are opaque, device-bound, rotated on every use, and reuse revokes the whole family (BUILD-PROMPT §4).

**State machines.** **User status**: `active -> suspended -> active`, `active -> deletion_pending (14-day cooling) -> deleted` (PII erased, financial rows pseudonymised, V77).

```mermaid
erDiagram
    users {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    user_identities {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
    }
    passkey_credentials {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
    }
    totp_credentials {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
    }
    otp_challenges {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    devices {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
    }
    session_families {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        uuid device_id FK
    }
    refresh_tokens {
        uuid id PK
        uuid family_id FK
    }
    age_attestations {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    age_blocks {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    addresses {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid geo_area_id FK
    }
    user_preferences {
        uuid user_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    store_follows {
        uuid user_id PK,FK
        uuid store_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    devices ||--o{ session_families : "device_id"
    session_families ||--o{ refresh_tokens : "family_id"
    stores ||--o{ store_follows : "store_id"
    users ||--o{ addresses : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ age_attestations : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ devices : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ passkey_credentials : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ session_families : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ store_follows : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ totp_credentials : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ user_identities : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ user_preferences : "user_id"
```

<a id="m-privacy"></a>
### 7.6 Consent and privacy (PDPL)

Consent is explicit, granular, never pre-ticked and withdrawable in one tap. SDKs and marketing channels start **only after** the matching purpose is granted; `consent_state` is the fast gate, `consent_records` the append-only proof. Purposes and texts are per market and versioned (ADR-0006 #3). DSAR export and deletion run as `privacy_requests`; financial and tax rows are pseudonymised, not deleted (V77). Breach handling tracks the PDPC 72-hour and subject 3-day clocks.

**State machines.** **PrivacyRequest**: `RECEIVED -> VERIFIED -> (BLOCKED | COOLING | IN_PROGRESS) -> COMPLETED`, `COOLING -> CANCELLED`, `RECEIVED -> REJECTED`. **Breach**: `DETECTED -> TRIAGED (<= 4 h) -> DPO_DECIDED -> PDPC_NOTIFIED (<= 72 h) -> SUBJECTS_NOTIFIED (<= 3 days) -> CLOSED`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    consent_purposes {
        text market_id PK,FK
        text code PK
        int version PK
    }
    consent_records {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        uuid device_id FK
    }
    consent_state {
        uuid user_id PK,FK
        text purpose_code PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    privacy_requests {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
        uuid export_media_id FK
    }
    breach_records {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    transfer_register {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    pii_access_log {
        uuid id PK
        uuid staff_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    retention_runs {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    devices ||--o{ consent_records : "device_id"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ pii_access_log : "staff_id"
    users ||--o{ consent_records : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ consent_state : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ privacy_requests : "user_id"
```

|---|---|---|---|---|
| `market_id` | text PK | no | P1 | -> `markets`. market scope (ADR-0006) |

<a id="m-sellers"></a>
### 7.7 Sellers, KYC, stores and staff

A **seller** is the legal entity (company or sole trader) that passes KYC and signs the Seller Agreement. A **store** is its storefront in one market; `store_id` is the tenant key for every seller-owned row (decision DM-07). One seller may run stores in several markets later (ADR-0006 #11). Staff are `users` linked by `store_members`; the Seller Center has a store switcher and the API sets **one** active store per transaction with `SET LOCAL app.current_store_id` (never per session, because PgBouncer runs in transaction mode).

Seller legal identity (legal name, commercial register number, tax card, governorate) is shown on every listing (CPL 181/2018, master prompt B5). For a sole trader those fields are personal data published by legal duty (V75).

**State machines.** **KYC case** (master prompt §19): `DRAFT -> SUBMITTED -> AUTO_CHECKS -> (APPROVED | NEEDS_INFO | REJECTED)`, `NEEDS_INFO -> SUBMITTED`, `APPROVED -> SUSPENDED -> APPROVED`.
**Store**: `DRAFT -> PENDING_REVIEW -> ACTIVE <-> VACATION`, `ACTIVE -> SUSPENDED -> ACTIVE` (human review within 5 business days, V15), `* -> CLOSED`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    sellers {
        uuid id PK
        text home_market_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid governorate_id FK
        text status
    }
    seller_signatories {
        uuid id PK
        uuid seller_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
    }
    seller_kyc_cases {
        uuid id PK
        uuid seller_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
        uuid decided_by FK
    }
    kyc_documents {
        uuid id PK
        uuid case_id FK
        uuid seller_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid media_id FK
    }
    kyc_checks {
        uuid id PK
        uuid case_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    stores {
        uuid id PK
        uuid seller_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid primary_category_id FK
        text status
    }
    store_settings {
        uuid store_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    store_storefronts {
        uuid store_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid logo_media_id FK
        uuid cover_media_id FK
        uuid custom_render_media_id FK
    }
    store_members {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        text role_code FK
        text status
        uuid invited_by FK
    }
    store_invites {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text role_code FK
        uuid created_by FK
    }
    store_locations {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid geo_area_id FK
    }
    payout_instruments {
        uuid id PK
        uuid seller_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    agreement_versions {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    agreement_acceptances {
        uuid id PK
        uuid agreement_version_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid accepted_by_user_id FK
    }
    brand_authorizations {
        uuid id PK
        uuid brand_id FK
        uuid seller_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid document_media_id FK
        text status
        uuid reviewed_by FK
    }
    agreement_versions ||--o{ agreement_acceptances : "agreement_version_id"
    brands ||--o{ brand_authorizations : "brand_id"
    categories ||--o{ stores : "primary_category_id"
    roles ||--o{ store_invites : "role_code"
    roles ||--o{ store_members : "role_code"
    seller_kyc_cases ||--o{ kyc_checks : "case_id"
    seller_kyc_cases ||--o{ kyc_documents : "case_id"
    sellers ||--o{ brand_authorizations : "seller_id"
    sellers ||--o{ kyc_documents : "seller_id"
    sellers ||--o{ payout_instruments : "seller_id"
    sellers ||--o{ seller_kyc_cases : "seller_id"
    sellers ||--o{ seller_signatories : "seller_id"
    sellers ||--o{ stores : "seller_id"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ brand_authorizations : "reviewed_by"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ seller_kyc_cases : "decided_by"
    stores ||--o{ store_invites : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ store_locations : "store_id"

    stores ||--o{ store_settings : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ store_storefronts : "store_id"
    users ||--o{ agreement_acceptances : "accepted_by_user_id"
    users ||--o{ seller_signatories : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ store_invites : "created_by"
    users ||--o{ store_members : "invited_by"
    users ||--o{ store_members : "user_id"
```

<a id="m-seller-plans"></a>
### 7.8 Seller plans and commission (SaaS)

Plans and commission are **configuration, not code** (owner decision D1 / Q3 is open). Commission resolution order, most specific wins: store contract override -> plan x category -> plan -> market default. The resolved rate is **snapshotted** on each seller order (`commission_bp_snapshot`) so later rate changes never touch past orders. Plan fees are netted from the seller balance when one exists, otherwise invoiced for bank transfer (ADR-0008). Entitlements are rows in `plan_entitlements`, checked by the policy package, never `if (plan === ...)`. A `max_commission_bp` config guard per market needs dual approval to exceed. ADR-0008 calls `seller_plan_subscriptions` `seller_subscriptions`.

**State machines.** **SellerPlanSubscription**: `TRIAL -> ACTIVE -> PAST_DUE -> (ACTIVE | ENDED)`, `ACTIVE -> CANCELLED_AT_PERIOD_END -> ENDED`. **PlanInvoice**: `OPEN -> (PAID | NETTED | VOID | UNCOLLECTIBLE)`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    seller_plans {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    commission_rules {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid plan_id FK
        uuid category_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
    }
    plan_entitlements {
        uuid plan_id PK,FK
        text key PK
    }
    seller_plan_subscriptions {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid plan_id FK
        text status
        uuid next_plan_id FK
    }
    seller_plan_invoices {
        uuid id PK
        uuid subscription_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
        uuid tax_document_id FK
    }
    categories ||--o{ commission_rules : "category_id"
    seller_plan_subscriptions ||--o{ seller_plan_invoices : "subscription_id"
    seller_plans ||--o{ commission_rules : "plan_id"
    seller_plans ||--o{ plan_entitlements : "plan_id"
    seller_plans ||--o{ seller_plan_subscriptions : "next_plan_id"
    seller_plans ||--o{ seller_plan_subscriptions : "plan_id"
    stores ||--o{ commission_rules : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ seller_plan_invoices : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ seller_plan_subscriptions : "store_id"
    tax_documents ||--o{ seller_plan_invoices : "tax_document_id"
```

<a id="m-catalog"></a>
### 7.9 Catalog: categories, brands, listings, variants, condition and defects

**Multi-category** taxonomy (fashion, electronics, home, kitchen, toys, beauty, pantry); launch categories are women's, modest and kids (Q7 default) but the model does not assume fashion. A product can sit in several categories (`product_categories`). Attributes are **JSONB validated against the category's attribute set** with a GIN index, plus promoted generated columns for the few hot facets (decision DM-04).

**Condition and defects (vision addendum §3).** Condition grade and the **defect card** live on the listing (`products`); every variant shares them. A different condition means a separate listing (UI spec §5). Defect cards are **immutable versions**: an edit creates a new version, and cart and order lines keep the version the shopper acknowledged. Critical (safety) defect types cannot be listed. Every non-new grade needs a real defect or condition photo before publish.

**Defect passport (feature 26).** Physical units of graded listings are serialised in `item_units`, each with a random 128-bit `passport_code` printed as a QR. The public passport page shows grade, defect card, photos and inspection date, never buyer data. This is separate from the reservation pool in `inventory_units` (decision DM-03); a one-off unit links pool row to passport through `inventory_units.item_unit_id` (ADR-0005 calls it `passport_id`).

**Names vs ADR-0009 (its data section is indicative; this ERD is authoritative):** `condition_reports` = `defect_cards` (versioned, per listing, and a listing never mixes conditions), `defects` = `defect_items`, `defect_passports` = `item_units` (passport status `active/sold/withdrawn` maps to `IN_STOCK|RESERVED`, `SOLD`, `WRITTEN_OFF|DONATED`), `defect_acknowledgements` = the `defect_ack_*` columns on `cart_lines` and `order_lines` (card version + time + count). Grade list and `stock_reason` follow ADR-0009.

**State machines.** **Product (listing)**: `DRAFT -> IN_REVIEW -> PUBLISHED <-> PAUSED`, `PUBLISHED -> SOLD_OUT -> PUBLISHED`, `* -> ARCHIVED`, `* -> BLOCKED` (T&S or enforcement). A price-check (Price Watch moment A) runs inside `IN_REVIEW -> PUBLISHED`.
**ItemUnit (passport)**: `IN_STOCK -> RESERVED -> SOLD -> (RETURNED -> RELISTED | WRITTEN_OFF | DONATED)`, `RESERVED -> IN_STOCK`.

<a id="m-engagement"></a>
### 7.10 Size profiles, reviews and see-it-first requests

MVP shopper features from the addendum: **My Size Only** feed (4), **fit-photo reviews** (11) and **WhatsApp video see-it-first** (19). Measurements are P3, need the `measurements` consent and are never visible to sellers. Reviews are verified-purchase only; the public projection shows "Verified buyer" plus size bought and rating, never a name unless the user opts in (so personal data does not travel to the global plane by default, V2).

```mermaid
        text market_id FK
    }

        uuid store_id FK
        text status
    }

        uuid id PK
        uuid review_id FK
        uuid media_id FK
        text status
    }
    see_it_first_requests {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid product_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text status
        uuid video_media_id FK
    }
    order_lines ||--o{ reviews : "order_line_id"
    products ||--o{ reviews : "product_id"
    products ||--o{ see_it_first_requests : "product_id"
    reviews ||--o{ review_media : "review_id"
    stores ||--o{ reviews : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ see_it_first_requests : "store_id"
    users ||--o{ reviews : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ see_it_first_requests : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ size_profiles : "user_id"
```

<a id="m-price-watch"></a>
### 7.11 Price integrity and Price Watch

Implements `docs/specs/price-watch.md` and master prompt §9. Sellers connect their own channel (Shopify, WooCommerce, feed URL; crawler in V2) after proving domain ownership and accepting the monitoring clause. Every internal or external price observation becomes a `price_events` row; `sku_price_state` keeps the 30-day lows and the **current display decision** for fast reads.

**VRP** = min(internal 30-day low, external 30-day low with match confidence >= tau). `discount_bp = floor((vrp - price) * 10000 / vrp)` in integer maths; a claim needs vrp present and discount >= 500 bp. Evidence older than 72 h fails closed to "no %". Brand display mode (public / members-only / hidden) only narrows what is shown; an unverified claim is never shown in any mode (addendum C4).

**State machines.** **PriceClaim**: `CURRENT -> SUPERSEDED` on every rescore, `CURRENT -> REVOKED` on stale evidence or enforcement. **ChannelConnection**: `PENDING_VERIFICATION -> CONNECTED <-> DEGRADED -> DISCONNECTED | REVOKED`. **Finding**: `OPEN -> (RESOLVED | APPEALED -> UPHELD | OVERTURNED)`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    channel_connections {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
        uuid consent_acceptance_id FK
    }
    external_products {
        uuid id PK
        uuid connection_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    channel_snapshots {
        uuid id PK
        timestamptz fetched_at PK
        uuid connection_id FK
        uuid external_product_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        bigint price_minor
        uuid screenshot_media_id FK
    }
    product_matches {
        uuid id PK
        uuid sku_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        uuid external_product_id FK
        text status
        uuid confirmed_by FK
    }
    price_events {
        uuid id PK
        timestamptz valid_from PK
        uuid sku_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid external_product_id FK
        bigint price_minor
    }
    sku_price_state {
        uuid sku_id PK,FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    price_claims {
        uuid id PK
        uuid sku_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        bigint price_minor
        text status
    }
    price_evidence {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid sku_id FK
        uuid screenshot_media_id FK
        uuid document_media_id FK
    }
    price_claim_evidence {
        uuid claim_id PK
        uuid evidence_id PK
        uuid store_id FK
    }
    price_findings {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid sku_id FK
        text status
    }
    enforcement_actions {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid finding_id FK
        uuid sku_id FK
        text status
        uuid reviewed_by FK
    }
    price_appeals {
        uuid id PK
        uuid finding_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid submitted_by FK
        uuid evidence_media_id FK
        uuid connection_id FK
        text status
    }
    price_check_runs {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid sku_id FK
        uuid requested_by FK
    }
    store_price_trust {
        uuid store_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    price_reports {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid sku_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text status
    }
    agreement_acceptances ||--o{ channel_connections : "consent_acceptance_id"
    channel_connections ||--o{ channel_snapshots : "connection_id"
```

| `scopes` | text[] | yes | P1 |  |
| `status` | text | no | P1 | see ChannelConnection machine |

<a id="m-inventory"></a>
### 7.12 Inventory: levels, row-per-unit pool, reservations

Master prompt §20.2 and ADR-0005: one row per sellable unit in a capped pool, reserved with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` in the **same Postgres** as the ledger, READ COMMITTED, SKUs locked in sorted order. A short pool (fewer than `qty` rows) rolls back the whole multi-SKU reservation. Claim on payment sets units `claimed`, increments `committed`, writes the outbox event and posts the ledger in one transaction. A sweeper (every 30 s, batches of 500, SKIP LOCKED) releases expired reservations. Redis holds only a "sold out" pre-filter. Reserve path: dedicated PgBouncer pool `checkout.reserve`, statement timeout 2 s, primary only.

**State machines.** **Reservation**: `ACTIVE -> CLAIMED | RELEASED | EXPIRED`. **InventoryUnit**: `available -> reserved -> (claimed | available)`.

<a id="m-cart"></a>
### 7.13 Bag and wishlists

One server-side bag per user per market, grouped by store in the UI, with a per-store free-delivery progress bar (threshold from `store_settings` or the market default). Max 50 lines, max 10 units per SKU. A line for a listing with a defect card cannot reach checkout until the shopper has ticked "I have seen the N defects" for **the current card version** (`defect_ack_*`). Wishlists support intents and **shared family wishlists** (feature 10) through members and an unguessable share token.

<a id="m-orders"></a>
### 7.14 Checkout and orders

One **OrderGroup** (one payment) -> many **SellerOrders** (one per store) -> **OrderLines**. Checkout is an orchestrated saga (master prompt §7): validate cart -> reserve -> hold credit -> create payment intent (`Idempotency-Key = order_group_id`) -> webhook -> claim + post ledger + outbox -> compensate on failure. Price-lock 10 min in `checkout_sessions`. Each line snapshots price, VAT, claim id, VRP and the acknowledged defect-card version (evidence trail for CPA inquiries). `funds_flow` is snapshotted per order group so both payment split models can coexist (decision DM-06, Q2).

Order tables are **not partitioned in Phase 1** (estimated 12-30M seller orders per year at 4M users). Trigger to partition by UUIDv7 id range: about 100M rows or vacuum lag, done by expand/contract (decision DM-12).

**State machines.** **OrderGroup**: `PENDING_PAYMENT -> (PAID | CONFIRMED_COD | PAYMENT_FAILED | EXPIRED)`, `PAID|CONFIRMED_COD -> (PARTIALLY_FULFILLED -> COMPLETED) | CANCELLED`.
**SellerOrder** (master prompt §20.1): `PENDING_PAYMENT -> PAID | CONFIRMED_COD -> ACCEPTED -> PACKED -> SHIPPED -> OUT_FOR_DELIVERY -> DELIVERED -> CLOSED` (after the return window); exceptions `CANCELLED`, `REJECTED_BY_SELLER`, `DELIVERY_FAILED -> RTO -> RETURNED_TO_SELLER`, `DISPUTED`. Return states live on `return_requests`.

<a id="m-payments"></a>
### 7.15 Payments, webhooks and settlement

Card data never touches us (PCI SAQ A): hosted page, iframe or SDK only. One payment intent per order group. Split tender = store credit (ledger hold) + one external method. **Both funds-flow models are supported** (Q2 open): in `psp_split`, the PSP splits per seller using `psp_merchant_accounts` and `payment_split_instructions`; in `collect_payout`, the platform collects and pays sellers through `payouts`. The ledger chart is the same; only the settlement path differs. Webhooks: IP allow-list + HMAC + timestamp +/-5 min, raw body stored encrypted, dedup on provider event id (`dedup_keys`).

**State machines.** **PaymentIntent**: `CREATED -> REQUIRES_ACTION -> AUTHORIZED -> CAPTURED`, `* -> FAILED | CANCELLED | EXPIRED`, `CAPTURED -> PARTIALLY_REFUNDED -> REFUNDED`. **SplitInstruction**: `PENDING -> SUBMITTED -> CONFIRMED | FAILED`, `CONFIRMED -> REVERSED`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    psp_merchant_accounts {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text status
    }
    payment_intents {
        uuid id PK
        uuid order_group_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        text status
    }
    payment_attempts {
        uuid id PK
        uuid intent_id FK
        text status
    }
    payment_tenders {
        uuid id PK
        uuid order_group_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        uuid payment_intent_id FK
        uuid ledger_hold_id FK
        text status
    }
    payment_split_instructions {
        uuid id PK
        uuid payment_intent_id FK
        uuid seller_order_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        uuid psp_merchant_account_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        text status
    }
    saved_payment_methods {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    payment_events_raw {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    settlement_files {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid file_media_id FK
        text status
    }
    settlement_lines {
        uuid id PK
        uuid file_id FK
        text market_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        text status
    }
    ledger_holds ||--o{ payment_tenders : "ledger_hold_id"
    order_groups ||--o{ payment_intents : "order_group_id"
    order_groups ||--o{ payment_tenders : "order_group_id"
    payment_intents ||--o{ payment_attempts : "intent_id"
    payment_intents ||--o{ payment_split_instructions : "payment_intent_id"
    payment_intents ||--o{ payment_tenders : "payment_intent_id"
    psp_merchant_accounts ||--o{ payment_split_instructions : "psp_merchant_account_id"
    seller_orders ||--o{ payment_split_instructions : "seller_order_id"
    settlement_files ||--o{ settlement_lines : "file_id"
    stores ||--o{ payment_split_instructions : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ psp_merchant_accounts : "store_id"
    users ||--o{ payment_intents : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ payment_tenders : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ saved_payment_methods : "user_id"
```

<a id="m-ledger"></a>
### 7.16 Ledger (double-entry)

Master prompt §T4 and BUILD-PROMPT §2. Every money movement is one `journal_entries` row plus >= 2 `postings` in one transaction. A **deferred constraint trigger** checks at COMMIT that postings sum to 0 per entry per currency. Postings and entries are **immutable** (UPDATE/DELETE/TRUNCATE revoked and blocked by trigger); corrections are reversing entries. Sign convention: positive = debit. Accounts are per currency and per market (ADR-0006 #2). Store-credit and gift-card value held during checkout is a `ledger_holds` row, not a pending posting, so postings stay immutable. Balances are projections reconciled nightly; postings are the truth.

**Partitioning (decision DM-05).** `journal_entries` is range-partitioned monthly on its UUIDv7 `id` (generated by the server at insert, so it equals booking time); `postings` uses the same bounds on `entry_id`, so an entry and its postings co-locate and the FK `postings(entry_id) -> journal_entries(id)` holds. The permanent business idempotency key lives in the small unpartitioned `journal_entry_keys`.

**Chart of accounts:** `docs/specs/ledger-chart-of-accounts.md` is authoritative for account types, worked examples and the id scheme `{type}:{market}:{currency}[:{owner}][:{sub}]` (for example `seller_pending:EG:EGP:<store>`, `seller_available:EG:EGP:<store>`, `store_credit:EG:EGP:<user>:refund_credit`). Here that string is `ledger_accounts.code` (UNIQUE, CHECK-built from the columns); postings reference the 16-byte UUID `id` to keep the largest table's keys small (decision DM-18).

**State machines.** **LedgerHold**: `HELD -> CAPTURED | RELEASED | EXPIRED`. **ReconciliationItem**: `UNMATCHED | AMOUNT_MISMATCH | MISSING_LEDGER | MISSING_SOURCE -> RESOLVED`; aging > 3 days escalates to Finance.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    ledger_account_types {
        text code PK
        text status
    }
    ledger_accounts {
        uuid id PK
        text type FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    journal_entries {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    journal_entry_keys {
        text idempotency_key PK
    }
    postings {
        uuid entry_id PK
        smallint line_no PK
        bigint amount_minor
    }
    ledger_holds {
        uuid id PK
        uuid account_id FK
        text market_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        text status
    }
    balance_projections {
        uuid account_id PK
        char(3) currency PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    ledger_seals {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    reconciliation_runs {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    reconciliation_items {
        uuid id PK
        uuid run_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    ledger_account_types ||--o{ ledger_accounts : "type"
    ledger_accounts ||--o{ ledger_holds : "account_id"
    reconciliation_runs ||--o{ reconciliation_items : "run_id"
```

<a id="m-payouts"></a>
### 7.17 Payouts

Seller earnings accrue per seller order and become eligible when the order is `CLOSED` (return window + 3 days) with no open dispute. Weekly cycle, dual approval above a threshold, IBAN name-match, idempotent provider call. Earnings move from `seller_pending` to `seller_available` at `CLOSED` (chart of accounts). A refund after payout makes `seller_available` negative and is netted from the next payout; plan fees (`seller_plan_invoices`) and penalties are netted too. In the `psp_split` model the PSP pays sellers directly and the payout row is a **statement** (`mode = statement`) that records the settlement instead of sending money.

**State machines.** **Payout** (master prompt §T4): `SCHEDULED -> ON_HOLD -> APPROVED -> SENT -> SETTLED | FAILED`, `SCHEDULED -> CANCELLED`. **Accrual**: `ACCRUING -> ELIGIBLE -> INCLUDED -> PAID`, `* -> ON_HOLD`, `PAID -> CLAWED_BACK`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    payout_accruals {
        uuid id PK
        uuid seller_order_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        text status
        uuid payout_id FK
    }
    payouts {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid payout_instrument_id FK
        text status
        uuid approval_request_id FK
    }
    payout_items {
        uuid id PK
        uuid payout_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        uuid accrual_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
    }
    payout_holds {
        uuid id PK
        uuid store_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid created_by FK
    }
    approval_requests ||--o{ payouts : "approval_request_id"
    payout_accruals ||--o{ payout_items : "accrual_id"
    payout_instruments ||--o{ payouts : "payout_instrument_id"
    payouts ||--o{ payout_accruals : "payout_id"
    payouts ||--o{ payout_items : "payout_id"
    seller_orders ||--o{ payout_accruals : "seller_order_id"
    staff_accounts ||--o{ payout_holds : "created_by"
    stores ||--o{ payout_accruals : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ payout_holds : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ payout_items : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ payouts : "store_id"
```

<a id="m-store-credit"></a>
### 7.18 Closed-loop store credit

ADR-0003 and master prompt §15.2: no top-up, no withdrawal, no P2P transfer; usable only on the platform; the UI says "credit", never "cash". Value enters only from refunds the shopper chose to take as credit and goodwill in Phase 1 (gift cards, cashback and referral credit are Phase 2). The **ledger is the balance truth**; `credit_lots` tracks expiry and spend order (soonest-expiring first, refund credit last) and is reconciled with postings nightly (V3).

**State machines.** **CreditLot**: `ACTIVE -> DEPLETED | EXPIRED | REVERSED`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    credit_lots {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid ledger_account_id FK
        text status
    }
    credit_lot_movements {
        uuid id PK
        uuid lot_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        uuid ledger_hold_id FK
    }
    credit_lots ||--o{ credit_lot_movements : "lot_id"
    ledger_accounts ||--o{ credit_lots : "ledger_account_id"
    ledger_holds ||--o{ credit_lot_movements : "ledger_hold_id"
    users ||--o{ credit_lot_movements : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ credit_lots : "user_id"
```

- Index: `(lot_id, id)`

- RLS: `user_id = current_setting('app.current_user_id')::uuid`, FORCE RLS.

<a id="m-returns"></a>
### 7.19 Returns, disputes and refunds

CPL 181/2018 baseline: windows count from courier-confirmed `delivered_at`; 14-day no-reason return also applies to defect items; a **disclosed and acknowledged** defect is not a defect reason; an undisclosed or worse-than-shown defect is a seller-fault return (30 days, seller pays) (addendum §3, V19). Inspection within 48 h of receipt, silence = auto-approve. The RefundRouter picks the route: card/wallet -> PSP to original; BNPL -> provider cancellation only; COD -> store credit instantly (shopper's choice) or bank transfer; store-credit portion -> same sub-balance. All refunds are ledger-first.

**State machines.** **Return**: `REQUESTED -> APPROVED -> PICKUP_SCHEDULED -> IN_TRANSIT -> RECEIVED -> INSPECTED -> (ACCEPTED | REJECTED -> DISPUTED) -> CLOSED`, `REQUESTED -> CANCELLED | REJECTED`.
**Dispute**: `OPEN -> UNDER_REVIEW -> (RESOLVED_SHOPPER | RESOLVED_SELLER) | WITHDRAWN` (human review, 5 business days).
**Refund** (master prompt §15.6): `REQUESTED -> APPROVED -> PROCESSING -> COMPLETED | FAILED (retry) | MANUAL_REVIEW`.

<a id="m-shipping"></a>
### 7.20 Shipping: domestic and international

Implements `docs/specs/shipping-domestic-international.md`. Weight is required for every SKU; size and HS code are required before publish if international is ticked. Chargeable weight per item = `max(actual_g, ceil(L*W*H mm^3 / divisor))` rounded **up** to the courier step; parcel weight = sum x qty + packaging allowance. All arithmetic is integer grams and integer minor units (volumetric divisor and step come from `courier_services`, V33). One parcel per store unless hub consolidation (Phase 2). Quotes are pure, locked 10 min with the price lock, and copied onto the seller order when used. Phase 1 = Egypt by zone and weight (Bosta, then Aramex). International export (DAP) is Phase 2; DDP and cross-border import are Phase 3.

**State machines.** **Shipment**: `LABEL_PENDING -> LABEL_CREATED -> PICKUP_SCHEDULED -> PICKED_UP -> IN_TRANSIT -> OUT_FOR_DELIVERY -> DELIVERED`, `OUT_FOR_DELIVERY -> DELIVERY_FAILED (3 attempts) -> RTO_IN_TRANSIT -> RETURNED_TO_SELLER`, `* -> LOST | CANCELLED`. Courier statuses are normalised to these.

<a id="m-tax"></a>
### 7.21 Tax engine and ETA

Tax engine per jurisdiction (ADR-0006 #5): rates per market and category in basis points, VAT-inclusive shelf prices, line-level net/VAT/gross in minor units, one banker's-rounding point. ETA is the first plug-in. The platform issues **B2B e-invoices to sellers** (commission, fees, plan fees) and **B2C e-receipts** for its own supplies (delivery fees it charges). Seller goods: the seller is the supplier; delegated e-receipt signing is optional and VERIFY (V6). Submission is a queue with idempotent internal numbers, status polling and a daily reconciliation.

**State machines.** **TaxDocument**: `DRAFT -> QUEUED -> SUBMITTED -> VALID | INVALID | REJECTED`, `VALID -> CANCELLED` (via credit note).

```mermaid
erDiagram
    tax_categories {
        text code PK
    }
    tax_rates {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text tax_category_code FK
    }
    seller_tax_profiles {
        uuid seller_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    tax_documents {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid issuer_seller_id FK
        text status
        uuid signed_doc_media_id FK
    }
    tax_document_lines {
        uuid tax_document_id PK,FK
        smallint line_no PK
    }
    eta_submissions {
        uuid id PK
        uuid tax_document_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    sellers ||--o{ seller_tax_profiles : "seller_id"
    sellers ||--o{ tax_documents : "issuer_seller_id"
    tax_categories ||--o{ tax_rates : "tax_category_code"
    tax_documents ||--o{ eta_submissions : "tax_document_id"
    tax_documents ||--o{ tax_document_lines : "tax_document_id"
```

<a id="m-promotions"></a>
### 7.22 Promotions, vouchers, Drop Hour and Outlet Parties

Deterministic promotions engine (master prompt §17): evaluation order item -> store -> order -> shipping -> payment-partner; at most one platform code plus one store voucher per store plus auto promos. Funding (platform, seller, co-funded) is recorded per line for the ledger and the VAT base. A voucher never creates a "% off" reference claim. **Budget data is never used for targeting** (lint rule). Hot budgets are spread over `promotion_budget_shards` so Drop Hour does not serialise on one counter row.

**Drop Hour and Outlet Parties** (features 2, 3, 32): a fixed daily drop per market time zone, themed parties and hidden-brand events from the market calendar. A seller cannot change price or stock after `SCHEDULED` except to increase stock. Per-user limits are enforced transactionally in `drop_purchase_counters`.

**State machines.** **Promotion**: `DRAFT -> SCHEDULED -> ACTIVE -> (PAUSED <-> ACTIVE) -> ENDED | EXHAUSTED`.
**Drop** (master prompt §16): `DRAFT -> SCHEDULED -> EARLY_ACCESS -> LIVE -> SOLD_OUT | ENDED -> ARCHIVED`.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    promotions {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text status
    }
    promotion_targets {
        uuid promotion_id PK,FK
        text target_type PK
        text target_id PK
    }
    promotion_budget_shards {
        uuid promotion_id PK,FK
        smallint shard PK
    }
    vouchers {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid promotion_id FK
        uuid assigned_user_id FK
        text status
    }
    voucher_redemptions {
        uuid id PK
        uuid voucher_id FK
        uuid promotion_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid order_group_id FK
        uuid seller_order_id FK
        bigint amount_minor
        text status
    }
    drops {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid calendar_event_id FK
        text state
    }
    drop_items {
        uuid drop_id PK,FK
        uuid sku_id PK,FK
        uuid store_id FK
        text status
    }
    drop_purchase_counters {
        uuid drop_id PK,FK
        uuid sku_id PK,FK
        uuid user_id PK,FK
    }
    drops ||--o{ drop_items : "drop_id"
    drops ||--o{ drop_purchase_counters : "drop_id"
    market_calendar_events ||--o{ drops : "calendar_event_id"
    order_groups ||--o{ voucher_redemptions : "order_group_id"
    promotions ||--o{ promotion_budget_shards : "promotion_id"
    promotions ||--o{ promotion_targets : "promotion_id"
    promotions ||--o{ voucher_redemptions : "promotion_id"
    promotions ||--o{ vouchers : "promotion_id"
    seller_orders ||--o{ voucher_redemptions : "seller_order_id"
    skus ||--o{ drop_items : "sku_id"
    skus ||--o{ drop_purchase_counters : "sku_id"
    stores ||--o{ drop_items : "store_id"
    stores ||--o{ promotions : "store_id"
    users ||--o{ drop_purchase_counters : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ voucher_redemptions : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ vouchers : "assigned_user_id"
    vouchers ||--o{ voucher_redemptions : "voucher_id"
```

<a id="m-risk"></a>
### 7.23 Fraud and COD risk

Rules plus (later) a GBDT score over the device/IP/instrument/address graph, COD refusal history, velocity and order value (master prompt §20.7). Decisions: allow, confirm by WhatsApp/IVR within 2 h, prepay the delivery fee, prepaid only, limit value, block. Identifiers in the graph are **keyed hashes only**. Automated restrictions need notice and a way to contest (V78).

**State machines.** **CodConfirmation**: `PENDING -> CONFIRMED | DECLINED | EXPIRED` (expired -> order auto-cancelled).

```mermaid
erDiagram
    risk_assessments {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    user_risk_profiles {
        uuid user_id PK,FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    cod_refusals {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        uuid shipment_id FK
        uuid seller_order_id FK
    }
    cod_confirmations {
        uuid id PK
        uuid order_group_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    risk_links {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid user_id FK
        uuid store_id FK
    }
    order_groups ||--o{ cod_confirmations : "order_group_id"
    seller_orders ||--o{ cod_refusals : "seller_order_id"
    shipments ||--o{ cod_refusals : "shipment_id"
    stores ||--o{ risk_links : "store_id"
    users ||--o{ cod_confirmations : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ cod_refusals : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ risk_assessments : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ risk_links : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ user_risk_profiles : "user_id"
```

<a id="m-notifications"></a>
### 7.24 Notifications

Channels: web push (Phase 1), in-app inbox, WhatsApp Business (template-approved), SMS, email; native push arrives with the apps in Phase 2. Marketing needs consent per channel; transactional does not. Frequency caps (marketing <= 1 push/day, <= 3/week) and quiet hours 23:00-09:00 market local time (transactional exempt) come from market config. Arabic first with EN fallback. Destinations are resolved at send time from encrypted user data; logs keep only a hash of the destination.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    notification_templates {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    notification_preferences {
        uuid user_id PK,FK
        text channel PK
        text category PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    notification_requests {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    notification_deliveries {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    in_app_messages {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    users ||--o{ in_app_messages : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ notification_preferences : "user_id"
    users ||--o{ notification_requests : "user_id"
```

<a id="m-search"></a>
### 7.25 Search sync and saved searches

The search engine is chosen by a bake-off (Typesense, Meilisearch, OpenSearch) on about 500 real Egyptian queries (BUILD-PROMPT §3). It runs on the **global public plane** and receives only P0 fields through public outbox events; staleness SLO < 60 s. Price and stock are re-checked from Postgres on the product page and in the bag. Postgres FTS is the fallback behind a kill switch. Query logs stay in the cell, without user ids.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    search_documents_state {
        text entity_type PK
        uuid entity_id PK
        text index_name PK
        text market_id FK
        text status
    }
    search_synonyms {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    saved_searches {
        uuid id PK
        uuid user_id FK
        text market_id FK
    }
    search_queries {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    users ||--o{ saved_searches : "user_id"
```

| `alert_enabled` | boolean | no | P1 |  |
| `last_run_at` | timestamptz | yes | P1 |  |
| `last_result_cursor` | text | yes | P1 |  |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | P1 | UTC |

- Index: `(user_id)`
- Index: `(last_run_at) WHERE alert_enabled` -> alert job
- RLS: `user_id = current_setting('app.current_user_id')::uuid`, FORCE RLS.

#### `search_queries`
Phase 1 · residency `cell` · tenant none · personal data · basis `legitimate_interest` · retention `search_logs_25m` · partition: RANGE (id) monthly

| Column | Type | Null | PII | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | uuid PK | no | P1 | partition key |
| `market_id` | text | no | P1 | -> `markets`. market scope (ADR-0006) |
| `locale` | text | no | P1 |  |
| `normalized_query` | text | no | P2 | scrubbed of phone numbers and emails; still treated as personal |
| `filters` | jsonb | yes | P1 |  |
| `result_count` | int | no | P1 |  |
| `no_result` | boolean | no | P1 | demand gaps |
| `latency_ms` | int | no | P1 |  |
| `engine` | text | no | P1 | primary or fallback |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | P1 | UTC |

- Index: `(no_result, id) WHERE no_result` -> demand-gap report
- No user or device id stored.
- ⚠ VERIFY: V76

<a id="m-seo"></a>
### 7.26 SEO: slugs, redirects, sitemaps, facets, feeds

BUILD-PROMPT §5. Paths are `/{market}-{lang}/...` (ADR-0006 #12) with hreflang clusters and no IP geo-redirect. Arabic slugs plus transliterated aliases that 301 to the canonical. A sold one-off unit returns 410 or 301 to the closest match; temporarily out of stock stays 200 with `OutOfStock`. Sitemaps are sharded at 50k URLs with `lastmod` from outbox events. Only an allow-list of category x facet pages is indexable. All tables here are public data, authoritative in the cell and projected to the edge.

```mermaid
erDiagram
    slugs {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    redirects {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    sitemap_entries {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
    }
    indexable_facet_pages {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid category_id FK
        text status
    }
    product_feed_runs {
        uuid id PK
        text market_id FK
        uuid file_media_id FK
        text status
    }
    categories ||--o{ indexable_facet_pages : "category_id"
```

#### `slugs`
Phase 1 · residency `cell_publishable` · tenant none · no personal data · basis `not_personal_data` · retention `catalog_life` · not partitioned

| Column | Type | Null | PII | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | uuid PK | no | P1 | UUIDv7, server-generated |
| `market_id` | text | no | P1 | -> `markets`. market scope (ADR-0006) |
| `locale` | text | no | P0 |  |
| `entity_type` | text | no | P0 | product, store, category, brand, facet_page, drop |
| `entity_id` | uuid | no | P0 |  |
| `slug` | text | no | P0 |  |
| `is_canonical` | boolean | no | P0 |  |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | P1 | UTC |
| `retired_at` | timestamptz | yes | P0 |  |

- Key: `UNIQUE (market_id, locale, entity_type, slug)`
- Key: `UNIQUE (market_id, locale, entity_type, entity_id) WHERE is_canonical`

#### `redirects`
Phase 1 · residency `cell_publishable` · tenant none · no personal data · basis `not_personal_data` · retention `catalog_life` · not partitioned

| Column | Type | Null | PII | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | uuid PK | no | P1 | UUIDv7, server-generated |
| `market_id` | text | no | P1 | -> `markets`. market scope (ADR-0006) |
| `from_path` | text | no | P0 |  |
| `to_path` | text | yes | P0 | NULL for 410 |
| `status_code` | smallint | no | P0 | 301, 308, 410 |
| `reason` | text | no | P0 | slug_change, transliteration_alias, sold_unit, merge |
| `created_at` | timestamptz | no | P1 | UTC |
| `expires_at` | timestamptz | yes | P0 |  |

- Key: `UNIQUE (market_id, from_path)`
- Chains are collapsed on write (A->B, B->C becomes A->C).

#### `sitemap_entries`
Phase 1 · residency `cell_publishable` · tenant none · no personal data · basis `not_personal_data` · retention `catalog_life` · not partitioned

| Column | Type | Null | PII | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `id` | uuid PK | no | P1 | UUIDv7, server-generated |
| `market_id` | text | no | P1 | -> `markets`. market scope (ADR-0006) |
| `locale` | text | no | P0 |  |
| `url_path` | text | no | P0 |  |
